  const CHAPTERS = [{"id":1,"title":"The Key","scenes":[{"prose":"The Flugzeug landed twelve minutes early.\n\nThat should have been my first Warnung. Nothing in my life had ever been early. Not my Geburt — I came out screaming two weeks late, according to my Großmutter — not my flights, not my Gefühle. But Berlin wanted me to arrive before I was bereit.\n\nI grabbed my Koffer. One bag. I had learned to travel like I had learned to love: leicht enough to leave fast. No photos, no gifts, no souvenirs that would break in my Rucksack. Just clothes, a phone charger, and a Reisepass with more stamps than I deserved.\n\nThe Flughafen was quiet for a Tuesday afternoon. A few families hugged at the Ankunftsbereich. A woman cried into her phone. A man in a too-thin coat leaned against a pillar, scrolling through his phone like he had all the Zeit in the world.\n\nHe was the one who called my name.\n\nThe man from the pillar. He wasn't tall. He wasn't smiling. His coat was grau, his hair was dunkel, and his eyes were the colour of the February sky outside — grey, but warm in a way that made me wütend. Why was he warm? I didn't know him.\n\n&quot;You're Matei,&quot; I said.\n\n&quot;You're late,&quot; he said.\n\nI looked at the clock. &quot;The plane was early. That's the opposite of late.&quot;\n\nHe didn't lachen. &quot;My train was late.&quot;\n\n&quot;So we're both enttäuscht.&quot;\n\nHe almost smiled. Fast. The corner of his mouth twitched like he had caught himself. Then he picked up my Koffer without asking — didn't even look at me for permission — and walked toward the Ausgang.\n\nThat's how it started. Me following a man who didn't ask permission.\n\nOutside, Berlin was grau and kalt and exactly as unforgiving as I needed it to be.\n\nThe Wind cut through my jacket like it had something to prove. I had been in cold weather before — Lagos could get chilly in December, if you squinted — but this was different. This was cold that wanted you to know you were a Gast here.\n\n&quot;Do you like it?&quot; Matei asked. He was already three steps ahead of me, my Koffer rolling behind him like a loyal dog.\n\n&quot;I don't know yet.&quot;\n\nHe said it like a Tatsache. Not a hope. A Tatsache. Like he had decided my opinion for me and was just waiting for me to catch up. I wanted to be annoyed. I was annoyed. But also — and I hated this — I wanted to believe him.\n\nWe took the Bus. He paid for my ticket without asking. I opened my mouth to protest, but he just looked at me and said, &quot;You don't have Euro yet.&quot; He was right. I closed my mouth.\n\nWe sat in the back, away from the other passengers. The Bus was almost leer. An old woman with a shopping cart. A teenager with headphones so large they looked like ear muffs. And us.\n\nHe smelled like Kaffee and cold smoke — the good kind, not cigarette smoke but something woodier, like a Kamin that had just been put out. It reminded me of someone I had never met. A person I had erfunden in my head when I was younger. Someone who would hold me without explanation.\n\n&quot;Why are you here?&quot; Matei asked. Not looking at me. Looking out the window at the grey buildings sliding past.\n\nI could have gelogen. I was good at lying. I had spent years telling my Großmutter I was fein, telling my coworkers I was glücklich, telling myself I wasn't running away. I had a whole catalogue of polite Lügen prepared. For work. To see the city. A fresh start. All true enough to pass.\n\nBut I was müde. And his eyes — when he finally turned to look at me — were warm.\n\n&quot;I wanted to leave.&quot;\n\n&quot;Leave from where?&quot;\n\nHe didn't say I'm sorry or that's sad or any of the things people say when they don't know what else to say. He just put his hand on mine. His hand was warm too.\n\nWe stayed like that for the rest of the Fahrt. Neither of us spoke. Neither of us pulled away. Outside, the city changed from airport glass to apartment blocks to old buildings with stories written in their cracked Putz. I watched it all through the window, but I didn't see any of it. I was too aware of his fingers against mine.\n\nWhen we got off the Bus, he let go. He didn't sich entschuldigen. He just pointed at a building across the street — five storeys, faded yellow paint, a door that had been painted over so many times it looked like a geology experiment. Bicycles chained to a railing. A bakery on the ground floor, closed for the night.\n\n&quot;That's my Wohnung,&quot; he said. &quot;You sleep on the Sofa.&quot;\n\nI looked at the building. Then at him.\n\n&quot;Only for one night.&quot;\n\n&quot;Only for one night,&quot; he agreed.","choices":["Proceed carefully, word by word","Step forward — trust your instincts"]},{"prose":"We both knew that was a Lüge. I don't know how I knew. Maybe it was the way he said it — too fast, like he was trying to convince himself. Maybe it was the way I didn't argue. But something in the air between us had already decided that one night wasn't going to be enough.\n\nThe stairs were eng and knarrten like they remembered every person who had ever climbed them.\n\nHis Wohnung was on the fourth floor. No Aufzug. By the time we got to his door, I was out of breath and he wasn't even sweating. Angeber.\n\nHe unlocked the door. Pushed it open. Stepped aside to let me in first.\n\nThe Wohnung was klein. That's the kindest thing I can say about it. One room that was supposed to be the living room, bedroom, and dining room all at once. A tiny kitchen in the corner, separated by a Theke with two stools. A door that I assumed led to a Badezimmer. One window that faced the street. One Lampe on a table that wobbled if you breathed on it. Two mismatched chairs — one wooden, one plastic. And a Sofa that had seen better decades. The fabric was worn thin in places, and one of the cushions had a dark Fleck that I decided not to investigate.\n\n&quot;It's not much,&quot; Matei said. He set my Koffer by the door and stood in the middle of the room with his hands in his pockets. For the first time, he looked nervös. Not scared — Matei didn't seem like the kind of person who got scared — but uncertain. Like he was waiting for me to judge him.\n\nHe nodded. Some of the Anspannung left his shoulders.\n\nI walked around the room, pretending to look at things. A Bücherregal crammed with paperbacks in German, Romanian, English. A Kalender on the wall with a photo of the Alps. A small stereo on the floor next to a stack of CDs — old ones, the kind I hadn't seen since I was a kid. Let me introduce it now because it is essential for B2.\n\nIn German, when you use weil (because), the verb goes to the end of the clause.\n\nEnglish order German order\n\nI stayed home because I was tired. Ich blieb zu Hause, weil ich müde war.\n\nI didn't sleep because I was nervous. In German, dass sends the verb to the end.\n\nI think that he remembers everything. Ich glaube, dass er sich an alles erinnert.\n\nShe said that I dropped a cup. Sie sagte, dass ich eine Tasse fallen gelassen habe.\n\nI know that you're scared. Ich weiß, dass du Angst hast.\n\nKey pattern: main clause + dass + subject + ... + verb (at the end).\n\nWhy this matters: In later chapters, characters will express thoughts and feelings with dass. Recognizing it will help you understand complex sentences without getting lost.\n\nTip: When you see dass, look for the verb at the very end of that clause. Small words that add tone, feeling, or attitude to a sentence. They don't translate directly to English. Very common in spoken German.\n\nKey particles you will encounter:\n\nja statement of obvious fact Das ist ja schön! (That's beautiful, as you can see)\n\ndoch contradiction / reminder Komm doch mit! (Just come with me — you know you should)\n\nhalt resignation / acceptance Das ist halt so. (That's just how it is)\n\neben same as halt Dann geh eben nicht. (Then just don't go)\n\nschon reassurance / qualification Das wird schon gut gehen. (It'll be fine — don't worry)\n\nmal softening / casual (short for einmal) Komm mal her. (Come here for a sec)\n\nExample from this chapter (paraphrased): &quot;Ich hab dir doch gesagt&quot; (I told you — remember?) — doch reminds the listener of something they should know.\n\nWhy this matters: Modal particles are the secret sauce of natural German. You don't need to produce them at B2, but recognizing them will make you understand tone much better. In the present tense, the prefix goes to the end. In the simple past (preterite/imperfect), the same rule applies — the prefix stays at the end, and the verb stem is conjugated.\n\nExamples from the story (present vs. past):\n\nVerb Present Simple Past (Imperfect)\n\nauspacken (to unpack) Ich packe aus Ich packte aus\n\nabschalten (to shut down) Ich schalte ab Ich schaltete ab\n\naufschließen (to unlock) Ich schließe auf Ich schloss auf\n\nankommen (to arrive) Ich komme an Ich kam an\n\nKey pattern: Conjugated verb stem + prefix at the end. Same word order as present tense!\n\nWhy this matters: As German density increases, you will see more simple past forms. Knowing they behave like present tense separable verbs (prefix at the end) helps you parse sentences faster. Also, Matei's internal monologue becomes much funnier in past tense. Instead of memorizing the case ending table, notice the patterns as you read. The most common endings are -e (nominative feminine/plural), -en (accusative masculine / dative plural), and -er (dative feminine).\n\nPractical tip: When you see mein, look at the noun after it. If the noun has an ending like -e or -en, the possessive will match. Your brain will absorb this naturally. Also, Matei uses possessives to claim things. Mein Kaffee. Mein Yves. Mein Notizbuch. Someone... quieter&quot; — the comparative would be leiser (quieter).\n\nComparative adjectives in German are formed by adding -er to the base adjective. Some take an umlaut (a → ä, o → ö, u → ü).\n\nCommon comparatives you will need:\n\nPositive Comparative English\n\nhübsch hübscher pretty → prettier\n\ngroß größer big → bigger (umlaut)\n\nklein kleiner small → smaller (no umlaut)\n\ngut besser good → better (irregular)\n\ngern lieber gladly → rather\n\nviel mehr much/many → more\n\nleise leiser quiet → quieter\n\nExample from this chapter: &quot;You're different than I expected. Someone... quieter.&quot; → Du bist anders, als ich erwartet habe. Jemand... leiser.\n\nUsing als for than: Mein Kaffee ist besser als deiner. (My coffee is better than yours.)\n\nWhy this matters: As you read more German, you will need to compare things. The comparative -er is regular for most adjectives, and you will recognize it easily once you have seen it a few times. Also, Matei uses comparatives to compliment Yves. Dein Kaffee ist besser als meiner. It's cute. Annoying. In past tense (imperfect/preterite), they are even more common in written German.\n\nThe six modal verbs in simple past:\n\nInfinitive Meaning Simple Past (ich)\n\nkönnen can, to be able to ich konnte\n\nmüssen must, to have to ich musste\n\nwollen to want to ich wollte\n\ndürfen may, to be allowed to ich durfte\n\nsollen should, to be supposed to ich sollte\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nI couldn't stop thinking. Recognizing konnte, musste, wollte helps you understand what characters could, had to, or wanted to do. Also, Matei uses wollte a lot when talking about Yves. Ich wollte dich nicht verlieren. It answers &quot;whose?&quot; In English, we use 's or of. In German, the article changes.\n\nDefinite article declension (genitive):\n\nmasculine des das Haus des Mannes (the man's house)\n\nneuter des das Buch des Kindes (the child's book)\n\nfeminine der die Farbe der Blume (the flower's color)\n\nplural der die Arbeit der Leute (the people's work)\n\nCommon genitive prepositions you will see:\n\nExamples from the IT world (relevant for your Ausbildung):\n\nthe company's server der Server des Betriebs\n\nthe applicant's qualifications die Qualifikationen des Bewerbers\n\nduring the interview während des Vorstellungsgesprächs\n\nWhy this matters: In everyday conversation, Germans often use von + dative instead of genitive (das Haus von dem Mann). But in formal writing — emails, letters, applications — the genitive is expected. You need to recognize it, even if you don't fully produce it.\n\nTip from the method: Don't memorize the table. Just notice that des appears before masculine/neuter nouns, and the noun often adds -s or -es. Your brain will absorb the pattern.","choices":["A memory surfaces — let it","Stay present in the moment"]}],"gate":{"clue":"What is the German word for \"rejection\"?","answer":"ablehnung","hint":"die Ablehnung"},"vocab":[{"german":"die Bewerbung","english":"application"},{"german":"die Ausbildung","english":"apprenticeship"},{"german":"der Betrieb","english":"company/firm"},{"german":"der Unterschied","english":"difference"},{"german":"das Anschreiben","english":"cover letter"},{"german":"der Lebenslauf","english":"CV/resume"},{"german":"das Vorstellungsgespräch","english":"job interview"},{"german":"die IHK","english":"Chamber of Commerce abbreviation"},{"german":"die Garamond","english":"Garamond (font)"},{"german":"typisch","english":"typical"},{"german":"ehrlich","english":"honest"},{"german":"praktisch","english":"practical"},{"german":"optimistisch","english":"optimistic"},{"german":"kreativ","english":"creative"},{"german":"die Emotion","english":"emotion"},{"german":"die Physik","english":"physics"},{"german":"der Serverraum","english":"server room"},{"german":"der Villain","english":"villain"},{"german":"das Handbuch","english":"handbook"},{"german":"die Vorbereitung","english":"preparation"}],"shadow":["Formal written German — practise the opening and closing of a professional letter:","Sehr geehrte Damen und Herren, — Dear Sir or Madam,","Hiermit bewerbe ich mich um die ausgeschriebene Ausbildungsstelle als Fachinformatiker. — I hereby apply for the advertised apprenticeship position as an IT specialist.","Ich freue mich auf ein persoenliches Gespraech. — I look forward to a personal meeting.","Mit freundlichen Gruessen — Kind regards","Hiermit (hereby) and Mit freundlichen Gruessen (kind regards) are not optional in formal German. Wegen der Ablehnung war ich traurig.\n\nDespite the rejection, I applied again. Trotz der Ablehnung habe ich mich wieder beworben.\n\nDuring the interview, I was nervous. Während des Vorstellungsgesprächs war ich nervös.\n\nWhy this matters: In everyday conversation, many Germans use von + dative instead (wegen von der Ablehnung). But in written German — emails, applications, formal letters — the genitive is correct. You need to recognize it, especially when reading emails from potential employers.\n\nTip from the method: Don't memorize the prepositions. Just notice that after wegen, trotz, während, the following article often changes (der → des for masculine/neuter, die → der for feminine/plural). In written German — especially narratives — the simple past (imperfect/preterite) is used instead of the present perfect.\n\nSimple past of sein (to be):\n\ner/sie/es war he/she/it was\n\nihr wart you (plural) were\n\nsie/Sie waren they/you (formal) were\n\nSimple past of haben (to have):\n\ner/sie/es hatte he/she/it had\n\nihr hattet you (plural) had\n\nsie/Sie hatten they/you (formal) had\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nHe was tired. (sich erinnern an)\n\nMany reflexive verbs in German are paired with specific prepositions. The preposition determines the case (usually accusative).\n\nCommon reflexive verb + preposition combinations:\n\nVerb Preposition Case Example\n\nsich erinnern an an (+ acc.) accusative Ich erinnere mich an den Schlüssel. (I remember the key.)\n\nsich freuen auf auf (+ acc.) accusative Ich freue mich auf das Wochenende. (I'm looking forward to the weekend.)\n\nsich freuen über über (+ acc.) accusative Ich freue mich über das Geschenk. (I'm happy about the gift.)\n\nsich kümmern um um (+ acc.) accusative Ich kümmere mich um die Pflanze. (I take care of the plant.)\n\nsich interessieren für für (+ acc.) accusative Ich interessiere mich für IT. (I'm interested in IT.)\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nI remember the almost smiles. Ich erinnere mich an die fast-Lächeln.\n\nI'm looking forward to seeing you. Ich freue mich auf dich.\n\nTake care of him. Kümmere dich um ihn.\n\nWhy this matters: In high-density German, you will see these reflexive-preposition combinations constantly. Instead of memorizing the list, notice the pattern: sich + verb + preposition + noun. The preposition links the verb to the thing being remembered, looked forward to, or cared for.\n\nTip from the method: When you see sich erinnern, look for an nearby. When you see sich freuen, look for auf (future) or über (past/present). Ich werde lernen.\n\nYou will succeed. Du wirst Erfolg haben.\n\nHe will stay. Er wird bleiben.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nYou will get the job. Du wirst den Job bekommen.\n\nWe will celebrate. Wir werden feiern.\n\nIt will be perfect. Es wird perfekt sein.\n\nWhy this matters: In spoken German, the present tense is often used for the future (Ich mache das morgen — I'll do that tomorrow). But in written German and formal contexts, the werden + infinitive construction appears frequently. Recognizing it will help you understand when something is going to happen.\n\nTip from the method: Don't overthink it. When you see werde, wirst, wird, werden, werdet, look for the infinitive at the end of the sentence. (Are you asking me?)\n\nSie (formal) fragen Fragen Sie mich? (Are you asking me?)\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nEnglish German (formal) German (informal)\n\nDo you have questions? In the novel, Yues uses Sie with the interviewers (correct) and du with Matei, Ada, Frau Schmidt (familiar).\n\nTip from the method: When in doubt, use Sie. It's better to be too formal than not formal enough. In perfect tense, the ge- goes between the prefix and the stem: aufgepasst, mitgenommen, zurückgekommen.\n\nExample from this chapter (implied): Ich komme nach Hause (I come home) → prefix nach goes to the end? No, nach Hause is a fixed phrase. But mitkommen — Kommst du mit? (Are you coming along?) — prefix mit at the end.\n\nWhy this matters: Recognizing separable verbs helps you guess meaning. Kommen = to come. Mitkommen = to come along (with). Zurückkommen = to come back. Ankommen = to arrive (come toward). The adjective takes the comparative form (-er).\n\nComparative forms you have seen in this novel:\n\nPositive Comparative Example\n\nschnell schneller Du lernst schneller als ich.\n\ngut besser Dein Kaffee ist besser als meiner.\n\nviel mehr Ich habe mehr Fehler gemacht als du.\n\nwenig weniger Er spricht weniger Deutsch als sie.\n\neinfach einfacher Die Prüfung war einfacher als gedacht.\n\nschwer schwerer Die nächste Prüfung wird schwerer sein.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nYou learn faster than I thought. Du lernst schneller als ich dachte.\n\nYour German is better than last month. Dein Deutsch ist besser als letzten Monat.\n\nI made more mistakes than you. Ich habe mehr Fehler gemacht als du.\n\nWhy this matters: You will use als constantly when comparing things — test scores, coffee quality, pastries, boyfriend annoyingness. The pattern is simple: adjective + er + als.\n\nTip from the method: Don't confuse als (than) with wie (as). Wie is for equality (so gut wie — as good as). Als is for inequality (besser als — better than). Both send the verb to the end.\n\nConjunction Meaning Type Example\n\ndass that fact Ich weiß, dass du recht hast.\n\nob whether uncertainty Ich weiß nicht, ob du recht hast.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nI knew that I would pass. Ich wusste, dass ich bestehen würde.\n\nI don't know whether I'll pass. Ich weiß nicht, ob ich bestehen werde.\n\nHe said that I should learn. Er sagte, dass ich lernen sollte.\n\nShe asked whether I was ready. Sie fragte, ob ich bereit war.\n\nWhy this matters: Dass and ob appear constantly in reported speech and thoughts. Recognizing the difference helps you understand whether someone is stating a fact (dass) or expressing uncertainty (ob).\n\nTip from the method: When you see ob, think &quot;whether&quot; or &quot;if&quot; (uncertain). When you see dass, think &quot;that&quot; (factual). Obwohl ich müde war, bin ich geblieben.\n\nI was tired; nevertheless, I stayed. Ich war müde; trotzdem bin ich geblieben.\n\nAlthough the test was hard, he passed. Obwohl der Test schwer war, hat er bestanden.\n\nThe test was hard; nevertheless, he passed. Der Test war schwer, trotzdem hat er bestanden.\n\nWhy this matters: Obwohl and trotzdem are everywhere in spoken German. They let you say &quot;yes, this bad thing happened, but I did the good thing anyway&quot; — which is exactly the theme of this chapter.\n\nTip from the method: Obwohl = verb at the end (like weil). Ich habe geübt, um den Antrag zu machen.\n\nHe left without saying goodbye. Er ging, ohne sich zu verabschieden.\n\nInstead of running, he stayed. Statt zu rennen, blieb er.\n\nWhy this matters: These structures are everywhere in spoken and written German. Recognizing um...zu helps you understand why someone does something.\n\nTip from the method: Um...zu always has a comma before um, and the zu goes directly before the infinitive verb. Both bevor and nachdem send the verb to the end.\n\nConjunction Meaning Verb position Example\n\nbevor before end Bevor du kommst, ruf mich an.\n\nnachdem after end (often with perfect/plusquamperfect) Nachdem sie gegangen war, weinte ich.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nBefore you come, call me. Bevor du kommst, ruf mich an.\n\nAfter she hung up, I sat in silence. Nachdem sie aufgelegt hatte, saß ich schweigend da.\n\nBefore I leave, I want to see you. Bevor ich gehe, will ich dich sehen.\n\nWhy this matters: You will use bevor and nachdem constantly when telling stories about past events — especially family phone calls.\n\nTip from the method: Nachdem is often used with the past perfect (hatte/waren + past participle) to show that one action happened completely before another. (subordinate) Ich bin glücklich, weil wir einen Garten haben.\n\nI'm happy because we have a garden. (main clause) Ich bin glücklich, denn wir haben einen Garten.\n\nWhy this matters: In spoken German, weil is more common. But denn is useful when you want a simpler word order (verb stays in second position, not at the end).\n\nTip from the method: If you're unsure about putting the verb at the end, use denn. Je länger wir warten, desto aufgeregter sind wir.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nThe longer we wait, the more excited we get. Je länger wir warten, desto aufgeregter sind wir.\n\nThe more we learn, the better parents we become. Je mehr wir lernen, desto bessere Eltern werden wir.\n\nThe more chaos, the more love. Je mehr Chaos, desto mehr Liebe.\n\nWhy this matters: Je...desto is common in spoken German when talking about growing feelings, expectations, or frustrations — like waiting for an adoption decision.\n\nTip from the method: The first clause (je) sets up the condition. The second clause (desto) gives the result. It sends the verb to the end.\n\nConjunction Meaning Example\n\nindem by (doing something) Er bestand die Prüfung, indem er viel lernte.\n\nExamples from this chapter (adapted):\n\nHe passed the exam by studying a lot. Er bestand die Prüfung, indem er viel lernte.\n\nBy staying, he found a home. Indem er blieb, fand er ein Zuhause.\n\nShe helped him by being patient. Sie half ihm, indem sie geduldig war.\n\nWhy this matters: Indem is common in written German and more formal speech. It allows you to explain how something happened.\n\nTip from the method: Indem always connects two clauses, and the verb in the indem clause goes to the end. Aber es hängt da. Und es bleibt da.&quot;\n\nDas Zertifikat hing neben der Tür. Gerahmt. Mit einem goldenen Rahmen. Zu groß. Zu protzig.\n\n&quot;Der Rahmen ist zu groß.&quot;\n\n&quot;Der Rahmen ist perfekt.&quot;\n\n&quot;Der Rahmen ist protzig.&quot;\n\n&quot;Du bist protzig. Dein Zertifikat auch.&quot;\n\n&quot;Das ist nicht wahr.&quot;\n\n&quot;Doch. Aber es ist dein Zertifikat. Du darfst protzig sein.&quot;\n\n&quot;Ich bin nicht protzig. Ein Mann.\n\nder Wecker alarm clock masculine (review)\n\ndas Jugendamt youth welfare office neuter (review)\n\ndie Zusage acceptance/confirmation feminine\n\ndie Absage rejection feminine\n\nder Krimi crime novel masculine\n\ndie Sarmale Romanian cabbage rolls plural\n\nder Löffel spoon masculine\n\nder Rahmen frame masculine\n\nFrom this chapter: &quot;Trotzdem. Meine Regeln gelten für immer&quot; (Nevertheless. My rules apply forever).\n\nObwohl and trotzdem express contrast:\n\nConjunction Word Order Example\n\nobwohl verb at end Obwohl der Basilikum tot ist, lieben wir den Garten.\n\ntrotzdem verb in 2nd position Der Basilikum ist tot, trotzdem lieben wir den Garten.\n\nWhy this matters: You made it through the whole novel. You've acquired more German than you know.","choices":["Notice the silence between them","Focus on what is being said"]}],"gate":{"clue":"What is the German word for \"hello\"?","answer":"hallo","hint":"Hallo"},"vocab":[{"german":"der Wecker","english":"alarm clock"},{"german":"das Jugendamt","english":"youth welfare office"},{"german":"die Zusage","english":"acceptance/confirmation"},{"german":"die Absage","english":"rejection"},{"german":"der Krimi","english":"crime novel"},{"german":"die Sarmale","english":"Romanian cabbage rolls"},{"german":"der Löffel","english":"spoon"},{"german":"der Rahmen","english":"frame"}],"shadow":["The last lines of the book.   // ── PROGRESS ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  const KEY = 'gj_v3';
  function loadProg(){
    try{ const r=localStorage.getItem(KEY); if(r) return {...defProg(),...JSON.parse(r)}; }catch(e){}
    return defProg();
  }
  function defProg(){ return {accessible:[1],cleared:[1],completed:[],ch:1,scene:0}; }
  function saveProg(p){ try{localStorage.setItem(KEY,JSON.stringify(p));}catch(e){} }
  let P = loadProg();

  function currentCh(){ return CHAPTERS.find(c=>c.id===P.ch)||CHAPTERS[0]; }

  // ── VOCAB LOOKUP ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function buildLookup(vocab){
    const map = new Map();
    const arts = ['der','die','das','ein','eine','einen','einem','einer','des','dem','den'];
    for(const v of vocab){
      const full = v.german.toLowerCase();
      map.set(full, v);
      const parts = v.german.split(/\s+/);
      if(parts.length > 1){
        const last = parts[parts.length-1].toLowerCase();
        if(!map.has(last)) map.set(last, v);
        if(arts.includes(parts[0].toLowerCase())){
          const noArt = parts.slice(1).join(' ').toLowerCase();
          if(!map.has(noArt)) map.set(noArt, v);
        }
      }
    }
    return map;
  }

  // ── TTS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function speak(text, lang='de-DE', rate=0.82, onEnd){
    if(!window.speechSynthesis){ onEnd&&onEnd(); return; }
    window.speechSynthesis.cancel();
    const u = new SpeechSynthesisUtterance(text);
    u.lang=lang; u.rate=rate;
    if(onEnd){ u.onend=onEnd; u.onerror=onEnd; }
    window.speechSynthesis.speak(u);
  }

  // ── TOOLTIP ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  let openTip = null;
  function closeTip(){
    if(openTip){ openTip.remove(); openTip=null; }
  }

  function makeGermanWord(rawWord, entry){
    const wrap = document.createElement('span');
    wrap.className='gw';
    wrap.textContent = rawWord;
    wrap.setAttribute('tabindex','0');
    wrap.setAttribute('role','button');
    wrap.setAttribute('aria-label', rawWord+': '+entry.english);

    function toggle(e){
      e.stopPropagation();
      if(openTip && wrap.contains(openTip)){ closeTip(); return; }
      closeTip();
      const tip = document.createElement('span');
      tip.className='tooltip';
      tip.innerHTML=`<span class="tt-word">${entry.german}</span>
        <span class="tt-tr">${entry.english}</span>
        <button class="tt-btn" aria-label="Pronounce">▶ Hear it</button>
        <button class="tt-close" aria-label="Close">×</button>`;
      tip.querySelector('.tt-btn').onclick=(ev)=>{ ev.stopPropagation(); speak(entry.german); };
      tip.querySelector('.tt-close').onclick=(ev)=>{ ev.stopPropagation(); closeTip(); };
      wrap.appendChild(tip);
      openTip=tip;
    }
    wrap.addEventListener('click', toggle);
    wrap.addEventListener('keydown', e=>{ if(e.key==='Enter'||e.key===' ') toggle(e); });
    return wrap;
  }

  document.addEventListener('click', ()=>closeTip());

  // ── PROSE RENDERER ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function renderProse(text, lookup){
    const div = document.createElement('div');
    div.className='prose-body';
    const paras = text.split(/\n\n+/).filter(p=>p.trim());
    for(const para of paras){
      const p = document.createElement('p');
      p.className='prose-para';
      const wordRe = /([A-Za-zäöüÄÖÜß]+)/g;
      let last=0, m;
      while((m=wordRe.exec(para))!==null){
        if(m.index>last) p.appendChild(document.createTextNode(para.slice(last,m.index)));
        const raw=m[1], entry=lookup.get(raw.toLowerCase());
        if(entry) p.appendChild(makeGermanWord(raw, entry));
        else p.appendChild(document.createTextNode(raw));
        last=m.index+raw.length;
      }
      if(last<para.length) p.appendChild(document.createTextNode(para.slice(last)));
      div.appendChild(p);
    }
    return div;
  }

  // ── CHAPTER MAP ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function buildMapDots(){
    const container = document.getElementById('chDots');
    container.innerHTML='';
    for(const ch of CHAPTERS){
      const btn=document.createElement('button');
      btn.className='ch-dot';
      const isCompleted=P.completed.includes(ch.id);
      const isAccessible=P.accessible.includes(ch.id);
      const isCurrent=P.ch===ch.id;
      if(isCurrent) btn.classList.add('current');
      else if(isCompleted) btn.classList.add('completed');
      else if(isAccessible) btn.classList.add('accessible');
      else btn.classList.add('locked');
      btn.textContent = isCompleted ? '✓' : ch.id;
      btn.title=ch.id+'. '+ch.title;
      btn.disabled=!isAccessible;
      btn.setAttribute('aria-label','Chapter '+ch.id+': '+ch.title);
      if(isAccessible){
        btn.onclick=()=>{ selectChapter(ch.id); closeMap(); };
      }
      container.appendChild(btn);
    }
  }

  function openMap(){ buildMapDots(); document.getElementById('mapOverlay').style.display='flex'; }
  function closeMap(){ document.getElementById('mapOverlay').style.display='none'; }

  // ── VOCAB PANEL ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function buildVocabPanel(vocab){
    const ul=document.getElementById('vocabList');
    ul.innerHTML='';
    for(const v of vocab){
      const li=document.createElement('li');
      li.className='vocab-item';
      const playBtn=document.createElement('button');
      playBtn.className='voc-play'; playBtn.textContent='▶';
      playBtn.onclick=()=>{ playBtn.textContent='◼'; speak(v.german,'de-DE',.82,()=>playBtn.textContent='▶'); };
      const de=document.createElement('span'); de.className='voc-de'; de.textContent=v.german;
      const sep=document.createElement('span'); sep.className='voc-sep'; sep.textContent='—';
      const en=document.createElement('span'); en.className='voc-en'; en.textContent=v.english;
      li.append(playBtn,de,sep,en);
      ul.appendChild(li);
    }
  }

  function openVocab(){ buildVocabPanel(currentCh().vocab); document.getElementById('vocabPanel').style.display='flex'; }
  function closeVocab(){ document.getElementById('vocabPanel').style.display='none'; }

  // ── SHADOWING ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  let shAutoOn=false, shActiveIdx=null, shPlayingWord=null;

  function buildShadowing(ch){
    document.getElementById('shTitle').textContent=ch.title;
    const script=document.getElementById('shScript');
    script.innerHTML='';
    shActiveIdx=null; shAutoOn=false;
    const autoBtn=document.getElementById('shAutoBtn');
    const repBtn=document.getElementById('shRepeatBtn');
    autoBtn.className='sh-auto'; autoBtn.textContent='▶ Auto-play';
    repBtn.disabled=true;

    for(let i=0;i<ch.shadow.length;i++){
      const line=ch.shadow[i];
      const dlgMatch=line.match(/^(Matei|Yves|Both|Narrator):\s*"?(.+?)"?\s*$/i);
      const isNote=/^(Read|Practise|Focus|Notice|That single|Say it)/i.test(line);

      if(dlgMatch){
        const div=document.createElement('div');
        div.className='sh-line sh-dialogue';
        div.dataset.idx=i;
        div.innerHTML=`<span class="sh-speaker">${dlgMatch[1]}</span>
          <span class="sh-text">"${dlgMatch[2]}"</span>
          <span class="sh-icon">▶</span>`;
        div.onclick=()=>{ if(!shAutoOn) shPlayLine(i); };
        script.appendChild(div);
      } else if(isNote){
        const div=document.createElement('div');
        div.className='sh-line sh-note';
        div.textContent=line;
        script.appendChild(div);
      }
    }
  }

  function shPlayLine(idx, thenNext=false){
    const ch=currentCh();
    const line=ch.shadow[idx];
    if(!line) return;
    shActiveIdx=idx;
    document.getElementById('shRepeatBtn').disabled=false;

    // Highlight active
    document.querySelectorAll('#shScript .sh-dialogue').forEach(el=>{
      el.classList.toggle('active', parseInt(el.dataset.idx)===idx);
      el.querySelector('.sh-icon').textContent = parseInt(el.dataset.idx)===idx ? '◼' : '▶';
    });

    const dlgMatch=line.match(/^(?:Matei|Yves|Both|Narrator):\s*"?(.+?)"?\s*$/i);
    const text = dlgMatch ? dlgMatch[1] : line;
    speak(text,'de-DE',0.75,()=>{
      document.querySelectorAll('#shScript .sh-dialogue').forEach(el=>{
        el.querySelector('.sh-icon').textContent='▶';
      });
      if(thenNext && shAutoOn){
        // find next dialogue index
        const allDlg=[...document.querySelectorAll('#shScript .sh-dialogue')].map(el=>parseInt(el.dataset.idx));
        const pos=allDlg.indexOf(idx);
        if(pos>=0 && pos<allDlg.length-1){
          setTimeout(()=>shPlayLine(allDlg[pos+1],true),900);
        } else {
          shAutoOn=false;
          const ab=document.getElementById('shAutoBtn');
          ab.className='sh-auto'; ab.textContent='▶ Auto-play';
        }
      }
    });
  }

  function toggleShAuto(){
    const ab=document.getElementById('shAutoBtn');
    if(shAutoOn){
      shAutoOn=false; window.speechSynthesis&&window.speechSynthesis.cancel();
      ab.className='sh-auto'; ab.textContent='▶ Auto-play';
    } else {
      shAutoOn=true;
      ab.className='sh-auto on'; ab.textContent='⏹ Stop';
      const allDlg=[...document.querySelectorAll('#shScript .sh-dialogue')].map(el=>parseInt(el.dataset.idx));
      const startIdx = (shActiveIdx!==null && allDlg.includes(shActiveIdx)) ? shActiveIdx : allDlg[0];
      if(startIdx!==undefined) shPlayLine(startIdx,true);
    }
  }

  function openShadowing(){ buildShadowing(currentCh()); document.getElementById('shadowSheet').style.display='flex'; }
  function closeShadowing(){ document.getElementById('shadowSheet').style.display='none'; shAutoOn=false; window.speechSynthesis&&window.speechSynthesis.cancel(); }

  // ── GATE VIEW ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function renderGate(ch){
    const main=document.getElementById('main');
    main.innerHTML='';
    let attempts=0;

    const gate=ch.gate;
    const div=document.createElement('div');
    div.className='gate';
    div.innerHTML=`
      <div class="gate-icon">🔐</div>
      <div class="gate-title">${ch.title}</div>
      <div class="gate-sub">One word unlocks the next chapter.</div>
      <div class="gate-clue">${gate.clue}</div>
      <div class="gate-hint" id="gateHint" style="display:none">Hint: <em>${gate.hint}</em></div>
      <div class="gate-row" id="gateRow">
        <input class="gate-input" id="gateInput" type="text" placeholder="type the German word…" autocomplete="off" spellcheck="false" autofocus>
        <button class="gate-submit" id="gateSubmit">Unlock</button>
      </div>
      <div class="gate-tries" id="gateTries"></div>
    `;
    main.appendChild(div);

    const input=document.getElementById('gateInput');
    const submit=document.getElementById('gateSubmit');
    const row=document.getElementById('gateRow');
    const triesEl=document.getElementById('gateTries');
    const hintEl=document.getElementById('gateHint');

    function norm(s){ return s.toLowerCase().trim().replace(/[^a-zäöüß]/g,''); }

    function check(){
      const val=norm(input.value);
      const ans=norm(gate.answer);
      if(!val) return;
      if(val===ans){
        input.className='gate-input ok';
        submit.disabled=true;
        setTimeout(()=>{
          P.cleared.push(ch.id);
          saveProg(P);
          renderChapter();
        },800);
      } else {
        attempts++;
        row.classList.add('shake');
        setTimeout(()=>row.classList.remove('shake'),500);
        input.value='';
        input.focus();
        if(attempts>=2) hintEl.style.display='';
        triesEl.textContent=attempts+' wrong '+(attempts===1?'attempt':'attempts')+' — keep going';
      }
    }

    submit.onclick=check;
    input.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{ if(e.key==='Enter') check(); });
    document.getElementById('footInfo').textContent='🔐 Vocab gate';
    updateFooterBtns();
  }

  // ── SCENE VIEW ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function renderScene(){
    const ch=currentCh();
    const scene=ch.scenes[Math.min(P.scene, ch.scenes.length-1)];
    const isLast=P.scene>=ch.scenes.length-1;
    const lookup=buildLookup(ch.vocab);
    const main=document.getElementById('main');
    main.innerHTML='';

    const article=document.createElement('div');
    article.className='chapter-article';

    // Pips
    const pips=document.createElement('div');
    pips.className='scene-pips';
    for(let i=0;i<ch.scenes.length;i++){
      const pip=document.createElement('div');
      pip.className='pip'+(i===P.scene?' active':i<P.scene?' done':'');
      pips.appendChild(pip);
    }
    article.appendChild(pips);

    // Prose
    const wrap=document.createElement('div');
    wrap.className='scene-wrap';
    const proseDom=renderProse(scene.prose, lookup);
    wrap.appendChild(proseDom);

    // Choices
    const choicesDiv=document.createElement('div');
    choicesDiv.className='choices';
    let chosen=false;

    function makeChoice(text,isFin){
      const btn=document.createElement('button');
      btn.className='choice-btn'+(isFin?' finish-btn':'');
      btn.innerHTML=`<span class="choice-arrow">${isFin?'✓':'›'}</span>${text}`;
      btn.onclick=()=>{
        if(chosen) return;
        chosen=true;
        btn.classList.add('selected');
        choicesDiv.querySelectorAll('.choice-btn').forEach(b=>b.disabled=true);
        setTimeout(()=>{
          if(isLast){
            // Complete chapter
            if(!P.completed.includes(ch.id)) P.completed.push(ch.id);
            const nextId=ch.id+1;
            if(!P.accessible.includes(nextId) && nextId<=35) P.accessible.push(nextId);
            saveProg(P);
            const nextCh=CHAPTERS.find(c=>c.id===nextId);
            if(nextCh){ selectChapter(nextId); }
            else { updateUI(); }
          } else {
            P.scene=Math.min(P.scene+1, ch.scenes.length-1);
            saveProg(P);
            renderScene();
          }
        },400);
      };
      return btn;
    }

    for(const c of scene.choices){
      choicesDiv.appendChild(makeChoice(c,false));
    }
    if(isLast){
      choicesDiv.appendChild(makeChoice('Complete this chapter',true));
    }

    wrap.appendChild(choicesDiv);
    article.appendChild(wrap);
    main.appendChild(article);

    requestAnimationFrame(()=>requestAnimationFrame(()=>wrap.classList.add('visible')));

    document.getElementById('footInfo').textContent=`Scene ${P.scene+1}/${ch.scenes.length}`;
    updateFooterBtns();
  }

  // ── CHAPTER SELECTION ─────────────────────────────────────────────
  function selectChapter(id){
    P.ch=id; P.scene=0; saveProg(P);
    renderChapter();
  }

  function renderChapter(){
    const ch=currentCh();
    document.getElementById('chapterLabel').textContent='Ch. '+ch.id+' — '+ch.title;
    document.getElementById('progressBadge').textContent=P.completed.length+'/35';
    if(P.cleared.includes(ch.id)) renderScene();
    else renderGate(ch);
    updateFooterBtns();
  }

  function updateFooterBtns(){
    const ch=currentCh();
    const prevBtn=document.getElementById('prevBtn');
    const nextBtn=document.getElementById('nextBtn');
    prevBtn.disabled=ch.id<=1;
    prevBtn.onclick=()=>{ if(ch.id>1){ const prev=CHAPTERS.find(c=>c.id===ch.id-1); if(prev&&P.accessible.includes(prev.id)) selectChapter(prev.id); } };
    const nextId=ch.id+1;
    nextBtn.disabled=nextId>35||!P.accessible.includes(nextId);
    nextBtn.onclick=()=>{ if(P.accessible.includes(nextId)) selectChapter(nextId); };
  }

  function updateUI(){ renderChapter(); }

  // ── EVENT LISTENERS ───────────────────────────────────────────────
  document.getElementById('mapBtn').onclick=openMap;
  document.getElementById('mapClose').onclick=closeMap;
  document.getElementById('mapOverlay').onclick=(e)=>{ if(e.target===document.getElementById('mapOverlay')) closeMap(); };

  document.getElementById('vocabBtn').onclick=openVocab;
  document.getElementById('vocabClose').onclick=closeVocab;

  document.getElementById('shadowBtn').onclick=openShadowing;
  document.getElementById('shadowClose').onclick=closeShadowing;
  document.getElementById('shAutoBtn').onclick=toggleShAuto;
  document.getElementById('shRepeatBtn').onclick=()=>{ if(shActiveIdx!==null) shPlayLine(shActiveIdx); };

  // Close panels on Escape
  document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{
    if(e.key==='Escape'){
      closeMap(); closeVocab(); closeShadowing();
    }
  });

  // ── INIT ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  renderChapter();

  })();
  </script>
  </body>
  </html>
